Shared Clinical and Immunological Features of Post-Acute COVID-19 and Post-Acute COVID-19 Vaccination Syndromes

Shared Clinical and Immunological Features of Post-Acute COVID-19 and Post-Acute COVID-19 Vaccination Syndromes
作者： Johannes Dinser, Moritz Anft, Lea Wiemers, Sarah Skrzypczyk, Eva Kohut, Julia Kurek, Ulrik Stervbo, Felix Sebastian Seibert, Timm H. Westhoff, Kamil Rosiewicz
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
Post-acute COVID-19 syndrome (PACS) can cause persistent disability involving fatigue, post-exertional malaise, neurocognitive symptoms, and autonomic manifestations. A clinically overlapping condition has been reported following COVID-19 vaccination and termed post-acute COVID-19 vaccination syndrome (PACVS), but its underlying mechanisms remain poorly understood.
设计与对象
这项横断面研究包括 28 名 PACS 患者、29 名 PACVS 患者和 16 名恢复期对照 (CCG)。
核心方法
使用 ME/CFS 相关症状问卷和贝尔评分评估临床表现。通过多色流式细胞术分析 T 细胞表型和对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 Spike、{β}1/{β}2-肾上腺素能受体和 M3/M4 毒蕈碱受体抗原反应的 T 细胞。通过 ELISA 定量循环 刺突蛋白 和 GPCR 自身抗体,并通过 qPCR 定量 EBV 负载。
主要结果
PACS 和 PACVS 显示出大量的临床重叠,尽管 PACS 中的一些症状更为严重。患者组之间的 GPCR 自身抗体谱相当,而与 CCG 相比,{β}2-肾上腺素能受体自身抗体有所增加。在未经调整的分析中,PACS 和 PACVS 之间的 57 个常规免疫表型参数中只有 3 个存在差异,而与 CCG 相比,两组均显示出多个改变。在两个患者组中均检测到循环 刺突蛋白,其中 PACVS 中 刺突蛋白 最高。与 CCG 相比,{β}1/{β}2 和 M3/M4 反应性 CD4+ T 细胞增加,而 Spike 特异性 CD4+ T 细胞减少。EBV 重新激活很少见。
解释与创新
PACS 和 PACVS 表现出大量的临床和免疫学重叠,包括 GPCR 指导的自身反应性和改变的受体反应性 T 细胞反应。这些共同的免疫介导特征表明重叠的病理生理机制,并有必要研究目前针对 PACS 探索的治疗策略是否也与 PACVS 相关。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果表明,免疫介导的机制可能是 PACS 和 PACVS 的基础,这可以为这些疾病的靶向治疗策略的开发提供信息。
局限与证据强度
该研究受到横断面设计和小样本量的限制,但证据充足,因为它直接比较了 PACS 和 PACVS 患者之间的免疫学特征。

Shared Clinical and Immunological Features of Post-Acute COVID-19 and Post-Acute COVID-19 Vaccination Syndromes

Authors: Johannes Dinser, Moritz Anft, Lea Wiemers, Sarah Skrzypczyk, Eva Kohut, Julia Kurek, Ulrik Stervbo, Felix Sebastian Seibert, Timm H. Westhoff, Kamil Rosiewicz
Original abstract (English)
Background: Post-acute COVID-19 syndrome (PACS) can cause persistent disability involving fatigue, post-exertional malaise, neurocognitive symptoms, and autonomic manifestations. A clinically overlapping condition has been reported following COVID-19 vaccination and termed post-acute COVID-19 vaccination syndrome (PACVS), but its underlying mechanisms remain poorly understood. We investigated whether immune dysregulation, autoreactivity, antigen persistence, and viral reactivation mechanisms implicated in PACS are also present in PACVS. Methods: This cross-sectional study included 28 PACS patients, 29 PACVS patients, and 16 convalescent controls (CCG). Clinical manifestations were assessed using ME/CFS-related symptom questionnaires and the Bell Score. T-cell phenotypes and T cells reactive to SARS-CoV-2 Spike, {beta}1/{beta}2-adrenergic receptor, and M3/M4 muscarinic receptor antigens were analyzed by multicolor flow cytometry. Circulating Spike protein and GPCR autoantibodies were quantified by ELISA, and EBV load by qPCR. Results: PACS and PACVS showed substantial clinical overlap, although several symptoms were more severe in PACS. GPCR autoantibody profiles were comparable between patient groups, while {beta}2-adrenergic receptor autoantibodies were increased compared with CCG. Only three of 57 conventional immunophenotyping parameters differed between PACS and PACVS in unadjusted analyses, whereas both groups showed multiple alterations compared with CCG. Circulating Spike protein was detected in both patient groups, with the highest prevalence in PACVS. {beta}1/{beta}2- and M3/M4-reactive CD4+ T cells were increased, whereas Spike-specific CD4+ T cells were reduced compared with CCG. EBV reactivation was rare. Conclusions: PACS and PACVS exhibit substantial clinical and immunological overlap, including GPCR-directed autoreactivity and altered receptor-reactive T-cell responses. These shared immune-mediated features suggest overlapping pathophysiological mechanisms and warrant investigation of whether therapeutic strategies currently explored for PACS may also be relevant to PACVS.

Towards an understanding of COVID-19-associated preeclampsia.

Towards an understanding of COVID-19-associated preeclampsia.
作者： A. Medel-Martinez, A. Cebollada-Solanas, M. Strunk, M. Fabre, V. M. Fernández-Soria, R. Ramos-Ruiz, C. Paules, D. Oros, J. Schoorlemmer, A. Medel-Martínez
摘要中文翻译简介:妊娠期间 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染与先兆子痫 (PE) 的较高发生率相关。虽然在 新型冠状病毒感染 妊娠中已有类似 PE 的胎盘异常的报道,但这种关联的分子机制仍不清楚。方法:我们对四个妊娠组的胎盘组织进行了批量 RNA 测序:正常流行前 (preCOVID-19)、流行前 PE (preCOVID-19 PE)、新型冠状病毒感染 无 PE (COVID no PE) 和 新型冠状病毒感染 并发 PE 或其他高血压疾病 (COVID PE)。使用 Deseq2 似然比检验 (LRT) 分析差异表达,并通过基因集富集分析确定失调途径。结果:对 LRT 显着差异表达基因 (DEG) 进行聚类,确定了四个妊娠组中的一组 16 个基因(由错误发现率调整后的 p 值 < 0.05 定义),显示出逐步表达变化——在 新型冠状病毒感染 之前的平静中最低,其次是在没有 PE 的 新型冠状病毒感染 中,然后是在 新型冠状病毒感染 之前的 PE 中,在 新型冠状病毒感染 中最高。该簇包括与胎盘功能障碍相关的基因,例如 LAMA5 和 BTNL9。虽然形态发生相关通路在所有病理组中均丰富,但免疫激活通路主要在 COVID PE 中富集,在 新型冠状病毒感染 前期 PE 中适度富集,而在 COVID 无 PE 中不存在。讨论:我们的研究结果确定了 新型冠状病毒感染 相关的胎盘特征,该特征反映了 PE 特征,并显示出定义胎盘应激连续体的进行性表达模式,母体 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染可能会加剧潜在的功能障碍。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
妊娠期间 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染与先兆子痫 (PE) 的较高发生率相关。该研究旨在探索这种关联背后的分子机制。
设计与对象
该研究对四个妊娠组的胎盘组织进行了批量 RNA 测序:正常流行前 (preCOVID-19)、流行前 PE (preCOVID-19 PE)、新型冠状病毒感染 无 PE (COVID no PE) 以及 新型冠状病毒感染 并发 PE 或其他高血压疾病 (COVID PE)。
核心方法
使用 Deseq2 似然比检验 (LRT) 分析差异表达,并通过基因集富集分析确定失调途径。
主要结果
LRT显著差异表达基因(DEGs)聚类分析在四个妊娠组中识别出一组16个基因(通过校正后的假发现率调整p值<0.05定义),显示出逐步的表达变化——在preCOVID-19无并发症妊娠组中最低,其次是新型冠状病毒感染感染但无子痫前期的妊娠组,然后是preCOVID-19子痫前期妊娠组,最后是新型冠状病毒感染相关子痫前期妊娠组的表达最高。
解释与创新
Our findings identify a COVID-19-associated placental signature that mirrors PE features and shows a progressive expression pattern defining a continuum of placental stress, with maternal SARS-CoV-2 infection potentially exacerbating underlying dysfunction.
科研与公卫意义
The findings provide insights into the molecular mechanisms linking SARS-CoV-2 infection to preeclampsia, which could inform future research on placental stress and guide targeted interventions for pregnant individuals with COVID-19.
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于胎盘组织分析,并未考虑其他潜在的混杂因素。该证据是中等的,因为它基于 RNA 测序和基因集富集分析,没有直接的临床结果。

Towards an understanding of COVID-19-associated preeclampsia.

Authors: A. Medel-Martinez, A. Cebollada-Solanas, M. Strunk, M. Fabre, V. M. Fernández-Soria, R. Ramos-Ruiz, C. Paules, D. Oros, J. Schoorlemmer, A. Medel-Martínez
Original abstract (English)
INTRODUCTION: SARS-CoV-2 infection during pregnancy has been associated with higher rates of preeclampsia (PE). While placental abnormalities resembling PE have been reported in COVID-19 pregnancies, the molecular mechanisms underlying the association remain unclear. METHODS: We conducted bulk RNA sequencing on placental tissues from four pregnancy groups: uneventful prepandemic (preCOVID-19), prepandemic PE (preCOVID-19 PE), COVID-19 without PE (COVID no PE), and COVID-19 complicated by PE or other hypertensive disorders (COVID PE). Differential expression was analyzed using Deseq2 likelihood ratio test (LRT), and gene set enrichment analysis identified dysregulated pathways. RESULTS: Clustering of LRT-significant differentially expressed genes (DEGs) identified a set of 16 genes (defined by the false discovery rate adjusted p-value < 0.05) across the four pregnancy groups, showing stepwise expression changes-lowest in preCOVID-19 uneventful, followed by those in COVID-19 without PE, then preCOVID-19 PE, and highest in COVID PE. This cluster included genes associated with placental dysfunction, such as LAMA5 and BTNL9. While morphogenesis-related pathways were enriched across all pathological groups, immune activation pathways were predominantly enriched in COVID PE, moderately in preCOVID-19 PE, and absent in COVID no PE. DISCUSSION: Our findings identify a COVID-19-associated placental signature that mirrors PE features and shows a progressive expression pattern defining a continuum of placental stress, with maternal SARS-CoV-2 infection potentially exacerbating underlying dysfunction.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
SARS-CoV-2 infection during pregnancy has been associated with higher rates of preeclampsia (PE). The study aimed to explore the molecular mechanisms underlying this association.
Design and population
The study conducted bulk RNA sequencing on placental tissues from four pregnancy groups: uneventful prepandemic (preCOVID-19), prepandemic PE (preCOVID-19 PE), COVID-19 without PE (COVID no PE), and COVID-19 complicated by PE or other hypertensive disorders (COVID PE).
Core methods
Differential expression was analyzed using Deseq2 likelihood ratio test (LRT), and gene set enrichment analysis identified dysregulated pathways.
Main results
Clustering of LRT-significant differentially expressed genes (DEGs) identified a set of 16 genes (defined by the false discovery rate adjusted p-value < 0.05) across the four pregnancy groups, showing stepwise expression changes-lowest in preCOVID-19 uneventful, followed by those in COVID-19 without PE, then preCOVID-19 PE, and highest in COVID PE.
Interpretation and novelty
Our findings identify a COVID-19-associated placental signature that mirrors PE features and shows a progressive expression pattern defining a continuum of placental stress, with maternal SARS-CoV-2 infection potentially exacerbating underlying dysfunction.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings provide insights into the molecular mechanisms linking SARS-CoV-2 infection to preeclampsia, which could inform future research on placental stress and guide targeted interventions for pregnant individuals with COVID-19.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to placental tissue analysis and does not account for other potential confounding factors. The evidence is moderate as it is based on RNA sequencing and gene set enrichment analysis without direct clinical outcomes.

Cysteine supplementation reverses immune dysfunction in cancer patients with severe COVID-19

Cysteine supplementation reverses immune dysfunction in cancer patients with severe COVID-19
作者： Tiffany Merlinsky, Jahan A. Rahman, William Thomas Johnson, Lisa McGary, Simon Grassmann, Jennifer Zhang, Abdulraouf Abdulraouf, Hannah L. Kalvin, Katherine Panageas, Jasmine Nicodemus
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The study aimed to understand the immunologic dysfunction in cancer patients with severe COVID-19 and to identify a therapeutic strategy to address this dysfunction. Severe COVID-19 in cancer patients is characterized by muted adaptive immune responses, and the molecular drivers of this dysfunction remain unclear.
设计与对象
The study involved cancer and non-cancer patients with or without COVID-19, comparing proteomic and metabolomic profiles to identify dysfunctional cysteine metabolism associated with severe COVID-19 in cancer patients.
核心方法
Integrated proteomic and metabolomic profiling was performed on matched cohorts of cancer and non-cancer patients with or without COVID-19 to identify dysfunctional cysteine metabolism.
主要结果
N-acetylcysteine (N-AC) treatment improved clinical outcomes in cancer patients with steroid-refractory COVID-19, reducing markers of innate inflammation, decreasing severe disease-associated MHC-II low monocytes, and increasing circulating CD8+ T cell abundance, activation, and effector differentiation.
解释与创新
N-AC 治疗 从机制上减少了抑制性单核细胞和 T 细胞之间前列腺素 E2 驱动的相互作用,从而实现克隆扩增和效应 T 细胞分化,并增强小鼠模型中肺浸润 CD8+ T 细胞的丰度。
科研与公卫意义
补充半胱氨酸 N-AC 可以在治疗上逆转严重呼吸道病毒感染中氧化还原驱动的免疫失调,特别是在高危癌症人群中,使其成为一种可行的治疗策略。
局限与证据强度
该研究因其对前瞻性临床试验和小鼠模型的依赖而受到限制,并且研究结果对其他患者群体的普遍性尚不确定。

Cysteine supplementation reverses immune dysfunction in cancer patients with severe COVID-19

Authors: Tiffany Merlinsky, Jahan A. Rahman, William Thomas Johnson, Lisa McGary, Simon Grassmann, Jennifer Zhang, Abdulraouf Abdulraouf, Hannah L. Kalvin, Katherine Panageas, Jasmine Nicodemus
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Severe COVID-19 infection in patients with cancer is characterized by a unique pattern of immunologic dysfunction including muted adaptive immune responses. The molecular drivers of immune dysfunction in cancer patients with severe COVID-19 remain unclear, and therapeutic strategies to overcome immune dysfunction in this setting have not been identified. We performed integrated proteomic and metabolomic profiling of matched cohorts of cancer and non-cancer patients with or without COVID-19 and identified dysfunctional cysteine metabolism as uniquely associated with severe COVID-19 in cancer patients. Treatment of patients with cancer and steroid-refractory COVID-19 with N-acetylcysteine in a prospective clinical trial ( NCT04374461 ) improved clinical outcomes compared with disease severity-matched hospitalized patients during the period immediately preceding clinical trial initiation. N-AC treatment reduced circulating markers of innate inflammation, decreased severe disease-associated MHC-II low monocytes, and increased circulating CD8+ T cell abundance, activation, and effector differentiation. Mechanistically, N-AC reduced prostaglandin E2-driven interactions between suppressive monocytes and T cells, which we confirmed was sufficient to limit T cell expansion and effector differentiation in a dose- and avidity-dependent fashion. Moreover, N-AC reduced the activity of inhibitory, redox sensitive transcription factors such as KLF6, enabling clonal expansion and effector T cell differentiation. We confirmed these observations in two murine models of severe respiratory viral infection, in which N-AC treatment significantly enhanced lung-infiltrating CD8+ T cell abundance. These findings establish cysteine supplementation as a viable therapeutic strategy to reverse redox-driven immune dysregulation in severe respiratory viral infection, particularly in the high-risk cancer population. One Sentence Summary Merlinsky et al show that cysteine supplementation can therapeutically reverse immune dysregulation in cancer patients with severe COVID-19 infection.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to understand the immunologic dysfunction in cancer patients with severe COVID-19 and to identify a therapeutic strategy to address this dysfunction. Severe COVID-19 in cancer patients is characterized by muted adaptive immune responses, and the molecular drivers of this dysfunction remain unclear.
Design and population
The study involved cancer and non-cancer patients with or without COVID-19, comparing proteomic and metabolomic profiles to identify dysfunctional cysteine metabolism associated with severe COVID-19 in cancer patients.
Core methods
Integrated proteomic and metabolomic profiling was performed on matched cohorts of cancer and non-cancer patients with or without COVID-19 to identify dysfunctional cysteine metabolism.
Main results
N-acetylcysteine (N-AC) treatment improved clinical outcomes in cancer patients with steroid-refractory COVID-19, reducing markers of innate inflammation, decreasing severe disease-associated MHC-II low monocytes, and increasing circulating CD8+ T cell abundance, activation, and effector differentiation.
Interpretation and novelty
N-AC treatment mechanistically reduced prostaglandin E2-driven interactions between suppressive monocytes and T cells, enabling clonal expansion and effector T cell differentiation, and enhanced lung-infiltrating CD8+ T cell abundance in murine models.
Scientific and public-health significance
Cysteine supplementation with N-AC can therapeutically reverse redox-driven immune dysregulation in severe respiratory viral infection, particularly in the high-risk cancer population, establishing it as a viable therapeutic strategy.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its reliance on a prospective clinical trial and murine models, and the generalizability of the findings to other patient populations is uncertain.

Association between the glucose-to-lymphocyte ratio and prognosis in COVID-19 patients

Association between the glucose-to-lymphocyte ratio and prognosis in COVID-19 patients
作者： Cong Shi, Sheng-Ping Gong
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
This study aimed to explore the associations between GLR and disease severity as well as clinical outcomes among hospitalized COVID-19 patients. The coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19) pandemic has transitioned into an endemic stage and is no longer classified as a public health emergency of international concern.
设计与对象
This retrospective cohort enrolled 235 hospitalized COVID-19 patients infected during the Omicron BA.5 and BF.7 wave from December 29, 2022 to January 15, 2023. All patients were followed up until the unified cutoff date of February 18, 2023, with all-cause mortality set as the primary endpoint.
核心方法
We measured inflammatory markers, the metabolic marker, and T-cell immune parameters to validate our a priori hypothesis that glucose-to-lymphocyte ratio (GLR) mirrors concurrent inflammation. We retrospectively analyzed a cohort of 235 individuals with COVID-19 to explore the prognostic value of GLR levels at diagnosis.
主要结果
我们的研究结果显示,重症患者的 GLR 水平显着高于轻中度患者。同时,高GLR组的β2-微球蛋白(β2-MG)、中性粒细胞与淋巴细胞比值(NLR)、血小板与淋巴细胞比值(PLR)和白细胞介素10(IL-10)显着升高,而载脂蛋白A1(ApoA1)、CD3绝对值、CD4绝对值和CD8绝对值显着降低。
解释与创新
Our data confirmed that GLR levels at initial diagnosis were significantly correlated with disease severity and prognosis in patients with COVID-19. According to multivariate Cox proportional hazards regression analysis, GLR ( P = 0.003) was found to be an independent, adverse prognostic factor for OS in Omicron patients.
科研与公卫意义
As an easily calculated metabolic-immune indicator, GLR acts as a promising complementary candidate biomarker for mortality risk stratification in Omicron COVID-19 patients. Further independent external validation is required before its routine clinical application.
局限与证据强度
两组仅部分重叠,因此高 GLR 并不等于严重疾病。所提供的证据并未报告用于评估 GLR 与临床结果之间关联的具体统计方法。

Association between the glucose-to-lymphocyte ratio and prognosis in COVID-19 patients

Authors: Cong Shi, Sheng-Ping Gong
Original abstract (English)
Background The coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19) pandemic has transitioned into an endemic stage and is no longer classified as a public health emergency of international concern. Nevertheless, the persistent emergence of novel variants with enhanced transmissibility and immune evasion potential suggests that the disease burden associated with COVID-19 remains substantial. This retrospective cohort enrolled 235 hospitalized COVID-19 patients infected during the Omicron BA.5 and BF.7 wave from December 29, 2022 to January 15, 2023. The prognostic value of biomarkers based on combined fasting glucose and lymphocyte counts in patients with COVID-19 has not been fully clarified. We measured inflammatory markers, the metabolic marker, and T-cell immune parameters to validate our a priori hypothesis that glucose-to-lymphocyte ratio (GLR) mirrors concurrent inflammation. This study aimed to explore the associations between GLR and disease severity as well as clinical outcomes among hospitalized COVID-19 patients. Methods We retrospectively analyzed a cohort of 235 individuals with COVID-19 to explore the prognostic value of GLR levels at diagnosis. All patients were followed up until the unified cutoff date of February 18, 2023, with all-cause mortality set as the primary endpoint. Two separate grouping methods were used: GLR stratification for evaluating its independent predictive ability, and severity grouping (mild-to-moderate vs severe) as clinical reference. The two groups only partially overlapped, so high GLR is not equivalent to severe illness. Patients were stratified into high GLR and low GLR groups. Overall survival (OS) was estimated via the Kaplan-Meier method and compared using the log-rank test. Results Our findings revealed that GLR levels were significantly higher in severe patients than in mild-to-moderate patients. Meanwhile, beta2-microglobulin (β2-MG), neutrophil-to-lymphocyte ratio (NLR), platelet-to-lymphocyte ratio (PLR), and interleukin-10 (IL-10) were significantly elevated in the high GLR group, whereas apolipoprotein A1 (ApoA1), CD3 absolute value, CD4 absolute value, and CD8 absolute value were significantly lower. Furthermore, patients with high GLR levels exhibited significantly shorter OS ( P 14.42) was found to be an independent, adverse prognostic factor for OS in Omicron patients, according to multivariate Cox proportional hazards regression analysis ( P = 0.003). Conclusions Our data confirmed that GLR levels at initial diagnosis were significantly correlated with disease severity and prognosis in patients with COVID-19. As an easily calculated metabolic-immune indicator, GLR acts as a promising complementary candidate biomarker for mortality risk stratification in Omicron COVID-19 patients; further independent external validation is required before its routine clinical application.

主动脉旁脂肪组织作为严重急性 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染的预测因子

Para-Aortic Adipose Tissue as Predictor of Severe Acute SARS-CoV-2 Infection
作者： Anna Bragina, Yulia Rodionova, Tatiana Ishina, Ivan Medvedev, Lubov Vasileva, Natalia Druzhinina, Karina Umbetova, Olga Belaya, Andrew Gorobchenko, Anna Khailova
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
Obesity is a well-established risk factor for severe COVID-19, largely mediated by chronic inflammation originating from adipose tissue. The role of para-aortic adipose tissue (PAT)—a component of perivascular adipose tissue in COVID-19 severity remains poorly understood.
设计与对象
This retrospective single-center cohort study included 279 hospitalized patients with confirmed COVID-19 pneumonia. Based on disease severity, patients were divided into moderate and severe groups.
核心方法
在单胸部计算机断层扫描上半自动测量主动脉周围的 PAT 体积。分析临床、实验室和影像数据。进行逻辑回归和受试者工作特征曲线分析以评估 PAT 体积的预测价值。
主要结果
Patients with severe COVID-19 were significantly older, had higher body mass index. PAT volume differed significantly between groups, with a greater proportion of higher values in the severe group. Severe cases also exhibited significantly elevated levels of C-reactive protein, ferritin, and D-dimer, and lower oxygen saturation.
解释与创新
Correlation analysis revealed significant associations between PAT volume and age, BMI, ferritin, the extent of lung involvement and SpO2. Multivariable logistic regression confirmed that log-transformed PAT volume was independently associated with severe COVID-19.
科研与公卫意义
PAT volume is independently associated with severe COVID-19, and values exceeding 0.35 cm3 may serve as a predictor of disease severity in hospitalized patients.
局限与证据强度
所提供的证据未报告该研究的局限性。

Para-Aortic Adipose Tissue as Predictor of Severe Acute SARS-CoV-2 Infection

Authors: Anna Bragina, Yulia Rodionova, Tatiana Ishina, Ivan Medvedev, Lubov Vasileva, Natalia Druzhinina, Karina Umbetova, Olga Belaya, Andrew Gorobchenko, Anna Khailova
Original abstract (English)
Background: /Objectives: Obesity is a well-established risk factor for severe COVID-19, largely mediated by chronic inflammation originating from adipose tissue. The role of para-aortic adipose tissue (PAT)—a component of perivascular adipose tissue in COVID-19 severity remains poorly understood. This study aimed to investigate the association between PAT volume and the severity of acute SARS-CoV-2 infection and to evaluate its potential as a predictor of severe COVID-19. Methods: This retrospective single-center cohort study included 279 hospitalized patients with confirmed COVID-19 pneumonia. Based on disease severity, patients were divided into moderate and severe groups. PAT volume was measured around the aorta semi-automatically on a single chest computed tomography. Clinical, laboratory, and imaging data were analyzed. Logistic regression and receiver operating characteristic curve analysis were performed to assess the predictive value of PAT volume. Results: Patients with severe COVID-19 were significantly older, had higher body mass index. PAT volume differed significantly between groups, with a greater proportion of higher values in the severe group. Severe cases also exhibited significantly elevated levels of C-reactive protein, ferritin, and D-dimer, and lower oxygen saturation. Correlation analysis revealed significant associations between PAT volume and age, BMI, ferritin, the extent of lung involvement and SpO2. Multivariable logistic regression confirmed that log-transformed PAT volume was independently associated with severe COVID-. The optimal threshold for initial PAT volume was 0.35 cm3 (sensitivity: 51.2%, specificity: 67.1%). Conclusions: PAT volume is independently associated with severe COVID-19, and values exceeding 0.35 cm3 may serve as a predictor of disease severity in hospitalized patients.

将噻唑/苯并噻唑基噻唑烷酮衍生物重新用作 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 主要蛋白酶 (Mpro) 抑制剂:综合计算和实验研究

Repurposing Thiazole/Benzothiazole-Based Thiazolidinone Derivatives as SARS-CoV-2 Main Protease (Mpro) Inhibitors: An Integrated Computational and Experimental Study
作者： Anthi Petrou, Aliki Papadimitriou-Tsantarliotou, Hathaichanok Chuntakaruk, Leentje Persoons, Thanyada Rungrotmongkol, Dominique Schols, Athina A. Geronikaki, Ioannis S. Vizirianakis, Steven De Jonghe
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
SARS-CoV-2, the causative agent of COVID-19, relies on its main protease (Mpro) for viral replication, making this enzyme an attractive target for antiviral drug development.
设计与对象
在本研究中,我们研究了先前合成的基于噻唑/苯并噻唑的噻唑烷酮衍生物库(最初开发为抗 HIV 药物)是否可以重新用作 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 Mpro 抑制剂。
核心方法
使用分子对接,针对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 Mpro 总共筛选了 85 种基于噻唑/苯并噻唑的噻唑烷酮衍生物。根据预测的结合亲和力,选择了十五个进行实验评估。使用酶测定来量化 Mpro 抑制,然后检查具有最明显酶活性的化合物在受到 Delta 和 Omicron 变体攻击的细胞培养模型中限制病毒复制的能力。
主要结果
分子对接鉴定了几种在 Mpro 活性位点内具有有利预测相互作用的衍生物。实验评估显示五种化合物对 Mpro 具有可测量的抑制活性,IC50 值范围为 0.19 至 13.15 μM。进一步的 抗病毒药物 测试表明,在针对 Delta 和 Omicron 变体的细胞检测中,一些活性化合物减少了病毒复制。
解释与创新
这些发现表明,基于噻唑/苯并噻唑的噻唑烷酮衍生物代表了 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 Mpro 抑制剂开发的有前途的化学支架。
科研与公卫意义
These findings may inform the development of new antiviral therapies targeting SARS-CoV-2 Mpro, potentially offering alternative treatments for COVID-19.
局限与证据强度
没有观察到酶抑制和 抗病毒药物 功效之间的直接相关性,表明其他药理学和细胞因素可能影响 抗病毒药物 活性。

Repurposing Thiazole/Benzothiazole-Based Thiazolidinone Derivatives as SARS-CoV-2 Main Protease (Mpro) Inhibitors: An Integrated Computational and Experimental Study

Authors: Anthi Petrou, Aliki Papadimitriou-Tsantarliotou, Hathaichanok Chuntakaruk, Leentje Persoons, Thanyada Rungrotmongkol, Dominique Schols, Athina A. Geronikaki, Ioannis S. Vizirianakis, Steven De Jonghe
Original abstract (English)
Background/Objectives: SARS-CoV-2, the causative agent of COVID-19, relies on its main protease (Mpro) for viral replication, making this enzyme an attractive target for antiviral drug development. In the present study, we investigated whether a library of previously synthesized thiazole/benzothiazole-based thiazolidinone derivatives, originally developed as anti-HIV agents, could be repurposed as SARS-CoV-2 Mpro inhibitors. Methods: A total of 85 thiazole/benzothiazole-based thiazolidinone derivatives were computationally screened against SARS-CoV-2 Mpro using molecular docking. Based on their predicted binding affinities, fifteen were selected for experimental evaluation. Enzymatic assays were used to quantify Mpro inhibition, and the compounds with the clearest enzymatic activity were then examined for their ability to limit viral replication in cell culture models challenged with the Delta and Omicron variants. Results: Molecular docking identified several derivatives with favorable predicted interactions within the Mpro active site. Experimental evaluation revealed five compounds with measurable inhibitory activity against Mpro, exhibiting IC50 values spanning 0.19 to 13.15 μM. Further antiviral testing demonstrated that some of the active compounds reduced viral replication in cell-based assays against both Delta and Omicron variants. However, a direct correlation between enzymatic inhibition and antiviral efficacy was not observed, indicating that additional pharmacological and cellular factors may influence antiviral activity. Conclusions: These findings demonstrate that thiazole/benzothiazole-based thiazolidinone derivatives represent a promising chemical scaffold for SARS-CoV-2 Mpro inhibitor development. Further optimization and mechanistic studies are warranted to improve their therapeutic potential against COVID-19.

严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 尖峰变体差异性地重新连接人类心肌细胞中的细胞骨架结构

SARS-CoV-2 Spike Variants Differentially Rewire Cytoskeletal Architecture in Human Cardiomyocytes
作者： Kellyann Román-Cruz, Calina Wu-Mo, Valerie Ortiz-Gómez, Emanuel A. Carrasquillo-Dones, Angélica Álvarez-Nieves, Jorge Rodríguez-Figueroa, Bismark A. Madera-Soto, Rafael Maldonado-Hernández, Jorge Rodriguez-Figueroa, Bismark Madera
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在研究不同变体的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 刺突蛋白 对人类心肌细胞的细胞骨架组织和蛋白质组景观的影响,因为这种流行病与心血管并发症有关。
设计与对象
该研究使用人类 AC16 心肌细胞,并评估了来自 Alpha、Delta 和 Omicron 变体的重组 刺突蛋白 在 24、48 和 72 小时暴露时间点的效果。
核心方法
使用共聚焦显微镜和定量图像分析来评估细胞骨架组织,包括 F-肌动蛋白丝计数和长度、鬼笔环肽荧光强度和细胞坚固性。在 72 小时暴露时间点采用基于定量串联质量标签 (TMT) 的蛋白质组学来表征 宿主 蛋白质丰度的变异相关变化。
主要结果
定量分析揭示了时间和变异依赖性的 F-肌动蛋白和形态重塑。所有三种尖峰变体在 48 小时时平均丝长度均显着减少,而所有变体暴露组的丝数量在 72 小时时出现显着减少。鬼笔环肽荧光强度和细胞坚固性进一步揭示了变异特异性反应,特别是在 72 小时时,Delta 和 Omicron 表现出不同的定量重塑模式。从形态学角度来看,暴露于 Delta 刺突的细胞在 72 小时时表现出最明显的整体细胞结构破坏。
解释与创新
作者解释说,观察到的细胞骨架和形态变化以及不同的蛋白质组学反应表明,不同的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体在心肌细胞中诱导独特的细胞反应,这可能导致心血管功能障碍。
科研与公卫意义
这些发现可能为未来关于 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 诱导的心脏功能障碍机制的研究提供信息,并可能对开发减轻长期心血管影响的靶向治疗或干预措施产生影响。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于使用人类心肌细胞进行体外分析,可能无法完全代表体内条件。该证据基于单一暴露时间点,并未考虑长期影响,因此证据强度中等。

SARS-CoV-2 Spike Variants Differentially Rewire Cytoskeletal Architecture in Human Cardiomyocytes

Authors: Kellyann Román-Cruz, Calina Wu-Mo, Valerie Ortiz-Gómez, Emanuel A. Carrasquillo-Dones, Angélica Álvarez-Nieves, Jorge Rodríguez-Figueroa, Bismark A. Madera-Soto, Rafael Maldonado-Hernández, Jorge Rodriguez-Figueroa, Bismark Madera
Original abstract (English)
Background/Objectives: The COVID-19 pandemic has resulted in more than 7.11 million deaths worldwide and has been associated with numerous extrapulmonary complications, including cardiovascular dysfunction. Although severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 (SARS-CoV-2) primarily targets the respiratory system, increasing evidence suggests that viral components may directly affect cardiac cells and contribute to long-term cardiovascular sequelae. In this study, we investigated the effects of recombinant spike proteins from the Alpha, Delta, and Omicron SARS-CoV-2 variants on the cytoskeletal organization and proteomic landscape of human AC16 cardiomyocytes. Methods: Confocal microscopy and quantitative image analysis were employed to evaluate cytoskeletal organization, including F-actin filament count and length, phalloidin fluorescence intensity, and cell solidity, following spike-protein exposure at 24, 48, and 72 h, while quantitative tandem mass tag (TMT)-based proteomics at the 72 h exposure time point was used to characterize variant-associated changes in host protein abundance. Results: Quantitative analysis revealed time- and variant-dependent F-actin and morphological remodeling. Mean filament length was significantly reduced by all three spike variants at 48 h, whereas significant reductions in filament count emerged at 72 h across all variant-exposed groups. Phalloidin fluorescence intensity and cell solidity further revealed variant-specific responses, particularly at 72 h, with Delta and Omicron exhibiting distinct quantitative remodeling patterns. Morphologically, Delta spike-exposed cells exhibited the most pronounced disruption of overall cellular architecture at 72 h. Proteomic analyses revealed distinct abundance patterns among proteins involved in cytoskeletal regulation, cell adhesion, membrane dynamics, mechanotransduction, and intracellular trafficking. Notably, Omicron was associated with broader changes in protein abundance, whereas Delta exhibited fewer protein-abundance changes despite showing the most pronounced morphological alterations. Collectively, these findings demonstrate time- and variant-dependent patterns of cytoskeletal and morphological remodeling, together with distinct variant-associated proteomic abundance patterns at the 72 h exposure time point. The divergence between the pronounced morphological alterations associated with Delta and the broader proteomic response induced by Omicron suggests that cytoskeletal, morphological, and molecular remodeling represent distinct but interconnected dimensions of the cardiomyocyte response to SARS-CoV-2 spike proteins. Conclusions: These findings identify cytoskeletal regulation and associated signaling pathways as potential contributors to SARS-CoV-2-associated cardiac cellular dysfunction.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to investigate the effects of SARS-CoV-2 spike proteins from different variants on the cytoskeletal organization and proteomic landscape of human cardiomyocytes, as the pandemic has been associated with cardiovascular complications.
Design and population
The study used human AC16 cardiomyocytes and evaluated the effects of recombinant spike proteins from the Alpha, Delta, and Omicron variants at 24, 48, and 72 h exposure time points.
Core methods
Confocal microscopy and quantitative image analysis were used to assess cytoskeletal organization, including F-actin filament count and length, phalloidin fluorescence intensity, and cell solidity. Quantitative tandem mass tag (TMT)-based proteomics was employed at the 72 h exposure time point to characterize variant-associated changes in host protein abundance.
Main results
Quantitative analysis revealed time- and variant-dependent F-actin and morphological remodeling. Mean filament length was significantly reduced by all three spike variants at 48 h, whereas significant reductions in filament count emerged at 72 h across all variant-exposed groups. Phalloidin fluorescence intensity and cell solidity further revealed variant-specific responses, particularly at 72 h, with Delta and Omicron exhibiting distinct quantitative remodeling patterns. Morphologically, Delta spike-exposed cells exhibited the most pronounced disruption of overall cellular architecture at 72 h.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted that the observed cytoskeletal and morphological changes, along with distinct proteomic responses, indicate that different SARS-CoV-2 variants induce unique cellular responses in cardiomyocytes, which may contribute to cardiovascular dysfunction.
Scientific and public-health significance
These findings may inform future research on the mechanisms of SARS-CoV-2-induced cardiac dysfunction and could have implications for the development of targeted therapies or interventions to mitigate long-term cardiovascular effects.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to in vitro analysis using human cardiomyocytes, which may not fully represent in vivo conditions. The evidence is based on a single exposure time point and does not account for long-term effects, thus the strength of the evidence is moderate.

发现 D 肽,通过稳定其单体状态以变构方式增强 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 3CLpro 活性。

Discovery of D-peptides that allosterically enhance SARS-CoV-2 3CLpro activity by stabilizing its monomeric state.
作者： Laiyi Feng, Xinliao Ling, Weijie Bian, Changsheng Zhang, Luhua Lai
摘要中文翻译严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 主蛋白酶 (3CLpro) 是病毒复制的必需酶,也是 抗病毒药物 药物开发的主要靶标。虽然已知其催化活性需要形成二聚体,但其从单体病毒多蛋白中裂解自身的机制仍有待进一步研究。在这项研究中,我们基于从头设计的 D-肽抑制剂设计了几种 D-肽。这些肽与 3CLpro 单体结合并意外地激活其催化功能。通过提高周转数,这些肽显着提高了 3CLpro 的催化效率。其中,LY11 以其高结合亲和力 (K D = 117 nM) 和强大的酶激活效力 (EC 200 < 1 μM) 脱颖而出。通过生物物理和计算方法,我们证明 LY11 以 1:1 化学计量结合,稳定单体状态,同时变构重塑 3CLpro 的域间连接子,以扩大底物结合袋并开启蛋白酶。这些发现为 3CLpro 的成熟和催化机制提供了新的见解。LY11 调节的 3CL 蛋白酶可作为合成生物学应用的多功能分子工具。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在研究 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 3CLpro 从单体病毒多蛋白中裂解自身的机制,因为已知其催化活性需要二聚体形成。
设计与对象
本研究涉及基于从头设计的 D-肽抑制剂的 D-肽工程,并分析它们对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 3CLpro 活性的影响。
核心方法
研究人员使用生物物理和计算方法证明 LY11 以 1:1 化学计量结合,稳定 3CLpro 的单体状态,同时变构重塑其域间连接子。
主要结果
D-肽与 3CLpro 单体结合并意外地激活其催化功能,显着提高蛋白酶的周转数和催化效率。
解释与创新
这些发现对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 3CLpro 的成熟和催化机制提出了新的见解,强调了该酶通过稳定其单体状态而意外激活。
科研与公卫意义
LY11 调节的 3CL 蛋白酶可以作为合成生物学应用的多功能分子工具,有可能为未来的 抗病毒药物 策略提供信息。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于体外研究结果,并未解决体内相关性,但由于使用了生物物理和计算方法,证据充足。

Discovery of D-peptides that allosterically enhance SARS-CoV-2 3CLpro activity by stabilizing its monomeric state.

Authors: Laiyi Feng, Xinliao Ling, Weijie Bian, Changsheng Zhang, Luhua Lai
Original abstract (English)
The SARS-CoV-2 main protease (3CLpro) is an essential enzyme for viral replication and a major target for antiviral drug development. While its catalytic activity is known to require dimer formation, the mechanism by which it cleaves itself from the monomeric viral polyprotein remains to be further studied. In this study, we engineered several D-peptides based on a de novo designed D-peptide inhibitor. These peptides bind to the 3CLpro monomer and unexpectedly activate its catalytic function. By enhancing the turnover number, the peptides significantly boost the catalytic efficiency of 3CLpro. Among them, LY11 stands out with high binding affinity (K D = 117 nM) and strong potency of enzymatic activation (EC 200 < 1 μM). Through biophysical and computational approaches, we show that LY11 binds in 1:1 stoichiometry, stabilizing the monomeric state while allosterically remodeling the inter-domain linker of 3CLpro to expand the substrate binding pocket and switch on the protease. These findings suggest new insights into the maturation and catalytic mechanism of 3CLpro. The LY11-regulated 3CL protease may serve as a versatile molecular tool for synthetic biology applications.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to investigate the mechanism by which SARS-CoV-2 3CLpro cleaves itself from the monomeric viral polyprotein, as its catalytic activity is known to require dimer formation.
Design and population
This study involved the engineering of D-peptides based on a de novo designed D-peptide inhibitor and the analysis of their effects on SARS-CoV-2 3CLpro activity.
Core methods
The researchers used biophysical and computational approaches to demonstrate that LY11 binds in 1:1 stoichiometry, stabilizing the monomeric state of 3CLpro while allosterically remodeling its inter-domain linker.
Main results
The D-peptides bind to the 3CLpro monomer and unexpectedly activate its catalytic function, significantly boosting the turnover number and catalytic efficiency of the protease.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings suggest new insights into the maturation and catalytic mechanism of SARS-CoV-2 3CLpro, highlighting an unexpected activation of the enzyme by stabilizing its monomeric state.
Scientific and public-health significance
The LY11-regulated 3CL protease may serve as a versatile molecular tool for synthetic biology applications, potentially informing future antiviral strategies.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to in vitro findings and does not address in vivo relevance, but the evidence is strong due to the use of biophysical and computational methods.

Journal: medRxiv  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-09-23  |  DOI: 10.64898/2026.09.21.26363608  |  Date basis: first_publication_date  |  Publication status: Current window  |  Citation: 2026
A 高优先级学术文献 · 综述A high priorityAcademic literature · Review

Childhood and adolescent immunisation intentions post-COVID-19 in Lusaka, Zambia

Childhood and adolescent immunisation intentions post-COVID-19 in Lusaka, Zambia
作者： M. Nyasa, Anjali Sharma, Andrew D. Kerkhoff, Dennis Ngosa, Bertha Shamoya, Kombatende Sikombe, Sandra Simbeza, Nelly Zulu, Elvin Hsing Geng, Jake M. Pry
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本研究旨在了解照顾者对针对儿童的新型疫苗接种疫苗的看法,以及他们在赞比亚卢萨卡地区对12-17岁儿童和青少年接种严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2疫苗的意愿。核心问题是观察到的严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2疫苗接种犹豫是否会扩展到未来针对儿童的新型疫苗疫苗。
证据基础与方法
本研究对一项横断面调查进行了二次数据分析,该调查于2023年11月13日至12月15日间在赞比亚卢萨卡的277名0-17岁儿童照顾者中进行。研究使用描述性统计方法探讨了人口统计学特征、疫苗接种疫苗接种史以及照顾者对严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2疫苗和新型疫苗疫苗的看法。
共识与结论
研究发现,93.1%的照顾者报告称其子女已接种所有推荐的常规儿童疫苗接种疫苗。照顾者最信任卫生部和医疗工作者作为疫苗疫苗信息的来源。然而,总体而言,进一步为儿童接种严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2疫苗的意愿较低,尤其是那些尚未接种的照顾者,有24.7%的照顾者表示没有为儿童接种严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2疫苗的意愿。
争议与缺口
研究指出,照顾者自身的疫苗接种疫苗接种状态与其青少年对严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2疫苗的接受度较高相关。然而,研究并未报告关于疫苗疫苗接种犹豫的具体机制或影响因素,也未提供对未来疫苗疫苗接种抗拒原因的详细见解。
科研与实践启示
通过卫生部和医疗工作者等可信赖来源进行有针对性和透明的沟通,可能是解决安全顾虑并增强对新型儿童疫苗疫苗信心的关键。本研究强调了需要加强对照顾者关于疫苗接种疫苗益处和风险的教育和宣传。

Childhood and adolescent immunisation intentions post-COVID-19 in Lusaka, Zambia

Authors: M. Nyasa, Anjali Sharma, Andrew D. Kerkhoff, Dennis Ngosa, Bertha Shamoya, Kombatende Sikombe, Sandra Simbeza, Nelly Zulu, Elvin Hsing Geng, Jake M. Pry
Original abstract (English)
Background: Since the COVID-19 pandemic, parental delays and refusals of well-established, safe, and effective childhood vaccinations have contributed to the resurgence of infectious diseases. This raises concerns that COVID-19 vaccine hesitancy may also extend to novel vaccines for children when introduced in the future. This study examined caregivers’, who included healthcare workers (HCWs), perceptions of novel vaccinations for children ages 0-17 years old and intentions to vaccinate children and adolescents ages 12-17 years against COVID-19. Methods: We conducted a secondary data analysis of a larger cross-sectional survey administered to parents/caregivers of children ages 0-17 years in Lusaka, Zambia, between 13 November and 15 December 2023. Vaccination intention for COVID-19 was specifically focused on caregivers of adolescents ages 12-17 years, following COVID-19 vaccination guidelines during the study. Descriptive statistics were used to explore demographic characteristics, vaccination history, and caregivers’ COVID-19 vaccination status, along with their children’s routine childhood vaccine histories and perceptions of COVID-19 and novel vaccinations. Results: Among 277 caregivers (66.8% women, median age: 35 years), 93.1% reported that their child had received all recommended routine childhood vaccinations. COVID-19 vaccine uptake was higher among HCWs (68.2% with ≥2 doses) than among community members (40.8%) and having ≥2 doses was associated with higher COVID-19 vaccine uptake among their adolescents ages 12-17 years. 58.0% of caregivers indicated that their 12-17 years old adolescents had received at least one COVID-19 vaccine dose. Overall intention to further vaccinate children against COVID-19, especially among those already unvaccinated, remained low, with 24.7% of caregivers reporting no intent to vaccinate their children. Protection from hospitalization and death from malaria, TB, HIV, pneumonia, influenza, and diarrheal diseases were the most cited reason (79.1%) for intention to vaccinate children with novel vaccines for these diseases. The most prominent reasons for reluctance towards future vaccines were the fear of potential side effects (45.5%) and insufficient knowledge about vaccination benefits and risks (31.8%). Caregivers most trusted the Ministry of Health (86.6%) and healthcare professionals (66.8%) as sources of vaccine information. Conclusions: While routine childhood vaccine uptake remained high, acceptance of COVID-19 and future novel vaccines for children was lower and was influenced by caregivers’ own vaccination status. Targeted and transparent communication through trusted sources such as the Ministry of Health and healthcare professionals may be critical for addressing safety concerns and strengthening confidence in novel childhood vaccines.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The study focused on understanding caregivers’ perceptions of novel vaccinations for children and their intentions to vaccinate children and adolescents ages 12-17 years against SARS-CoV-2 in Lusaka, Zambia. The central question was whether the hesitancy observed with the SARS-CoV-2 vaccine would extend to future novel vaccines for children.
Evidence base and method
The review conducted a secondary data analysis of a cross-sectional survey of 277 caregivers of children aged 0-17 years in Lusaka, Zambia, collected between 13 November and 15 December 2023. The study used descriptive statistics to explore demographic characteristics, vaccination history, and caregivers’ perceptions of SARS-CoV-2 and novel vaccines.
Consensus and conclusions
The study found that 93.1% of caregivers reported their child had received all recommended routine childhood vaccinations. Caregivers most trusted the Ministry of Health and healthcare professionals as sources of vaccine information. However, overall intention to further vaccinate children against SARS-CoV-2, especially among those already unvaccinated, remained low, with 24.7% of caregivers reporting no intent to vaccinate their children.
Controversies and gaps
The study noted that caregivers’ own vaccination status was associated with higher acceptance of SARS-CoV-2 vaccines for their adolescents. However, the evidence does not report on the specific mechanisms or factors influencing vaccine hesitancy, nor does it provide detailed insights into the reasons for reluctance towards future vaccines.
Research and practice implications
Targeted and transparent communication through trusted sources such as the Ministry of Health and healthcare professionals may be critical for addressing safety concerns and strengthening confidence in novel childhood vaccines. The study highlights the need for improved education and outreach to caregivers about the benefits and risks of vaccination.

Rethinking non-human primate models for emerging viral infections after COVID-19

Rethinking non-human primate models for emerging viral infections after COVID-19
作者： Dong‐Yeon Kim, Jung Joo Hong
摘要中文翻译当问题需要对系统病毒动力学、保护性免疫、组织病理学或纵向结果进行综合分析时,非人类灵长类动物 (NHP) 在新兴病毒感染研究中具有特殊价值。然而,它们的成本、有限的可用性、专业化管理和道德限制阻碍了它们作为默认筛查平台的常规使用。我们以严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒 2 (严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2) 为案例研究,比较了恒河猴、食蟹猴、非洲绿猴、狒狒和狨猴对 发病机制、疫苗 保护、治疗效果、免疫记忆和急性后结局研究的贡献。我们强调,这些物种与问题的关系不能不加批判地转移到未来的病原体;必须根据受体的使用、组织向性、疾病表型和实验目的重新建立模型的适用性。人工智能引导的预测、人类类器官和器官芯片系统可以通过优先考虑变体和候选者、识别组织特异性机制以及在 NHP 研究开始之前完善终点来支持这一过程。因此,我们提出了一个问题驱动的工作流程,其中计算和人类相关平台在适合目的的 NHP 验证之前缩小了证据差距。该策略可以提高可解释性、重现性以及与 3R 的一致性,同时保留 NHP 用于需要完整生物体水平生物学的问题。

Rethinking non-human primate models for emerging viral infections after COVID-19

Authors: Dong‐Yeon Kim, Jung Joo Hong
Original abstract (English)
Non-human primates (NHPs) retain particular value in emerging viral infection research when questions require integrated analysis of systemic viral kinetics, protective immunity, tissue pathology, or longitudinal outcomes. However, their cost, limited availability, specialized husbandry, and ethical constraints preclude their routine use as default screening platforms. Using severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 (SARS-CoV-2) as a case study, we compare how rhesus macaques, cynomolgus macaques, African green monkeys, baboons, and marmosets have contributed to studies of pathogenesis, vaccine protection, therapeutic efficacy, immune memory, and post-acute outcomes. We emphasize that these species-question relationships cannot be transferred uncritically to future pathogens; model suitability must be re-established according to receptor usage, tissue tropism, disease phenotype, and experimental objective. AI-guided prediction, human organoids, and organ-on-chip systems can support this process by prioritizing variants and candidates, identifying tissue-specific mechanisms, and refining endpoints before NHP studies begin. We therefore propose a question-driven workflow in which computational and human-relevant platforms narrow the evidence gap before fit-for-purpose NHP validation. This strategy can improve interpretability, reproducibility, and alignment with the 3Rs while preserving NHP use for questions that require intact organism-level biology.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Non-human primates (NHPs) retain particular value in emerging viral infection research when questions require integrated analysis of systemic viral kinetics, protective immunity, tissue pathology, or longitudinal outcomes.
Evidence base and method
However, their cost, limited availability, specialized husbandry, and ethical constraints preclude their routine use as default screening platforms.
Consensus and conclusions
Using severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 (SARS-CoV-2) as a case study, we compare how rhesus macaques, cynomolgus macaques, African green monkeys, baboons, and marmosets have contributed to studies of pathogenesis, vaccine protection, therapeutic efficacy, immune memory, and post-acute outcomes.
Controversies and gaps
We emphasize that these species-question relationships cannot be transferred uncritically to future pathogens; model suitability must be re-established according to receptor usage, tissue tropism, disease phenotype, and experimental objective.
Research and practice implications
AI-guided prediction, human organoids, and organ-on-chip systems can support this process by prioritizing variants and candidates, identifying tissue-specific mechanisms, and refining endpoints before NHP studies begin.

COVID-19, immunity, and vaccination: unravelling the interplay of sex, gender, age, and the exposome

COVID-19, immunity, and vaccination: unravelling the interplay of sex, gender, age, and the exposome
作者： Anna Calabrò, Giulia Accardi, Emanuele Montomoli, Graham Pawelec, Claudia Maria Trombetta, Calogero Caruso
摘要中文翻译性别和性别相关差异深刻影响 免疫应答 到 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染和 疫苗接种,但在研究和公共卫生策略中仍未得到充分考虑。本综述探讨了性别、性别、年龄和暴露组如何相互作用来影响对冠状病毒 19 疾病 (新型冠状病毒感染) 的易感性、疾病严重程度、长新冠 和 疫苗 诱导的免疫。男性患严重疾病、住院和 死亡率 的比率较高,而女性通常具有更强的先天性和适应性 死亡率,从而有助于增强恢复能力。这些差异源于生物因素和性别相关决定因素之间复杂的相互作用,包括职业暴露、医疗保健获取、健康相关行为、社会经济条件和微生物群,这些因素都会影响个体暴露。这篇综述还强调了衰老对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 免疫力的影响,强调了在百岁老人中观察到的保留的免疫调节,这可能有助于在高龄时预防严重的 新型冠状病毒感染。讨论了 长新冠 的当前证据,包括女性中较高的 患病率 以及可能导致持续症状的性别特异性免疫途径。我们检查了 疫苗 反应中与年龄、性别和性别相关的差异,区分功效和效果。尽管女性通常会产生更强的抗体反应,但这种优势并不一定转化为更大的临床保护。疫苗接种 对男性的相对益处似乎更大,因为他们患严重疾病的基线风险更高。最后,我们讨论了暴露因素(包括生活方式、微生物群组成以及环境和社会决定因素)如何影响 疫苗 的反应性和犹豫性。整合性别、性别、年龄和暴露视角对于制定更公平和个性化的策略来预防和管理 新型冠状病毒感染 和未来新出现的疾病至关重要。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
性别和性别相关差异深刻影响 免疫应答 到 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染和 疫苗接种,但在研究和公共卫生策略中仍未得到充分考虑。
证据基础与方法
本综述探讨了性别、性别、年龄和暴露组如何相互作用来影响对冠状病毒 19 疾病 (新型冠状病毒感染) 的易感性、疾病严重程度、长新冠 和 疫苗 诱导的免疫。
共识与结论
男性患严重疾病、住院和 死亡率 的比率较高,而女性通常具有更强的先天性和适应性 死亡率,从而有助于增强恢复能力。讨论了 长新冠 的当前证据,包括女性中较高的 患病率 以及可能导致持续症状的性别特异性免疫途径。我们检查了 疫苗 反应中与年龄、性别和性别相关的差异,区分功效和效果。
争议与缺口
这些差异源于生物因素和性别相关决定因素之间复杂的相互作用,包括职业暴露、医疗保健获取、健康相关行为、社会经济条件和微生物群,这些因素都会影响个体暴露。疫苗接种 对男性的相对益处似乎更大,因为他们患严重疾病的基线风险更高。尽管女性通常会产生更强的抗体反应,但这种优势并不一定转化为更大的临床保护。
科研与实践启示
Integrating sex, gender, age, and the exposome perspectives is essential for developing more equitable and personalised strategies to prevent and manage COVID-19 and future emerging diseases. Finally, we discuss how exposomic factors, including lifestyle, microbiota composition, and environmental and social determinants, influence vaccine responsiveness and hesitancy.

COVID-19, immunity, and vaccination: unravelling the interplay of sex, gender, age, and the exposome

Authors: Anna Calabrò, Giulia Accardi, Emanuele Montomoli, Graham Pawelec, Claudia Maria Trombetta, Calogero Caruso
Original abstract (English)
Sex- and gender-related differences profoundly influence immune responses to SARS-CoV-2 infection and vaccination but remain insufficiently considered in research and public health strategies. This review explores how sex, gender, age, and the exposome interact to shape susceptibility to coronavirus-19 disease (COVID-19), disease severity, Long COVID, and vaccine-induced immunity. Males experience higher rates of severe disease, hospitalisation, and mortality, whereas females generally mount stronger innate and adaptive immune responses, contributing to greater resilience. These differences arise from a complex interplay between biological factors and gender-related determinants, including occupational exposures, healthcare access, health-related behaviours, socioeconomic conditions, and the microbiota, which contribute to the individual exposome. This review also highlights the impact of ageing on SARS-CoV-2 immunity, highlighting the preserved immune regulation observed in centenarians, which may contribute to protection against severe COVID-19 despite advanced age. Current evidence on Long COVID is discussed, including its higher prevalence among females and the sex-specific immunological pathways that may underlie persistent symptoms. We examine age-, sex-, and gender-related differences in vaccine response, distinguishing efficacy from effectiveness. Although females generally mount stronger antibody responses, this advantage does not necessarily translate into greater clinical protection. The relative benefit of vaccination appears greater in males because of their higher baseline risk of severe disease. Finally, we discuss how exposomic factors, including lifestyle, microbiota composition, and environmental and social determinants, influence vaccine responsiveness and hesitancy. Integrating sex, gender, age, and the exposome perspectives is essential for developing more equitable and personalised strategies to prevent and manage COVID-19 and future emerging diseases.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question

Controversies and gaps

The relative benefit of vaccination appears greater in males because of their higher baseline risk of severe disease. Although females generally mount stronger antibody responses, this advantage does not necessarily translate into greater clinical protection.
Research and practice implications
Integrating sex, gender, age, and the exposome perspectives is essential for developing more equitable and personalised strategies to prevent and manage COVID-19 and future emerging diseases. Finally, we discuss how exposomic factors, including lifestyle, microbiota composition, and environmental and social determinants, influence vaccine responsiveness and hesitancy.

Serious cardiac events in the COVID-19 era in the paediatric population: what did we learn?

Serious cardiac events in the COVID-19 era in the paediatric population: what did we learn?
作者： Darsh Safi, Kayanne Toutounji, Mariam Toufic Arabi
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
严重的心脏事件通常与心肌炎或心律失常相关,引起严重的公共卫生问题。这篇叙述性综述比较了成人和儿童的严重心脏事件 发病率、机制和结果。
证据基础与方法
Data were gathered from peer-reviewed studies between 2019 and 2025, such as systematic reviews, clinical reports, and cohort analyses. Focus was placed on the potential contribution of COVID-19-related cardiac events.
共识与结论
While vaccine-related cardiac complications are still uncommon, COVID-19 infection is linked to an increased risk of serious cardiac events in adults. Paediatric serious cardiac events, on the other hand, are rare but frequently associated with multisystem inflammatory syndrome in children, and there have been rare, documented vaccine-associated deaths in this population. Significant findings reveal clear patterns in serious cardiac events mechanisms, with age-dependent differences in the severity and prognosis of COVID-19-related myocarditis, arrhythmias, and long QT syndrome, among others.
争议与缺口
Important knowledge gaps about the long-term cardiac effects of COVID-19 still exist. These lead to the possibility of unusual or delayed cardiac consequences and the requirement for specialised monitoring techniques to reduce potential risks, particularly in high-risk populations.
科研与实践启示
Key research and clinical practice gaps were identified by analysing paediatric and adult populations in terms of diagnostic methods, management techniques, and long-term results. These variations highlight the necessity of age-appropriate risk assessment and management techniques. In the context of COVID-19, these findings highlight distinct risk profiles for serious cardiac events, underscoring the need for targeted screening methods and focused public health initiatives.

Serious cardiac events in the COVID-19 era in the paediatric population: what did we learn?

Authors: Darsh Safi, Kayanne Toutounji, Mariam Toufic Arabi
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: Serious cardiac events, which are frequently associated with myocarditis or arrhythmias, pose serious public health concerns. COVID-19 can increase cardiovascular risks by causing cardiac damage, arrhythmias, and multisystem inflammatory syndrome in children. However, important knowledge gaps about the long-term cardiac effects of COVID-19 still exist. These lead to the possibility of unusual or delayed cardiac consequences and the requirement for specialised monitoring techniques to reduce potential risks, particularly in high-risk populations. This narrative review compares serious cardiac event incidence, mechanisms, and outcomes in adults and children. METHODS: Data were gathered from peer-reviewed studies between 2019 and 2025, such as systematic reviews, clinical reports, and cohort analyses. Focus was placed on the potential contribution of COVID-19-related cardiac events. Key research and clinical practice gaps were identified by analysing paediatric and adult populations in terms of diagnostic methods, management techniques, and long-term results. RESULTS: While vaccine-related cardiac complications are still uncommon, COVID-19 infection is linked to an increased risk of serious cardiac events in adults. Paediatric serious cardiac events, on the other hand, are rare but frequently associated with multisystem inflammatory syndrome in children, and there have been rare, documented vaccine-associated deaths in this population. CONCLUSION: Significant findings reveal clear patterns in serious cardiac events mechanisms, with age-dependent differences in the severity and prognosis of COVID-19-related myocarditis, arrhythmias, and long QT syndrome, among others. These variations highlight the necessity of age-appropriate risk assessment and management techniques. In the context of COVID-19, these findings highlight distinct risk profiles for serious cardiac events, underscoring the need for targeted screening methods and focused public health initiatives.

严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染和 长新冠 临床谱中的新兴生物标志物

Emerging Biomarkers Across the Clinical Spectrum of SARS-CoV-2 Infection and Long COVID
作者： Akhilesh Koli, Ayush Khangar, Sharmilla Khatri, Poonam V. Suryawanshi, Srikanth Tripathy
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
为了保持一致性,本次审查自始至终使用术语“长新冠”;PASC 和 PACS 仅在反映特定引用研究使用的术语时才会注明。
证据基础与方法
这篇叙述性综述综合了与 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染相关的候选生物标志物的证据,特别关注它们与 长新冠 的机制、临床和转化相关性。
共识与结论
Reflecting the fact that many candidate markers have been characterised primarily in acute or severe COVID-19, we explicitly indicate, for each biomarker domain, the strength and directness of the evidence linking it to long COVID as opposed to acute disease severity alone, and we distinguish exploratory candidates from those with more established clinical utility.
争议与缺口
相对于之前对 长新冠 生物标志物的系统综述,该综述的显着贡献在于其明确区分了急性期和慢性期证据,将遗传、表观遗传和多组学数据整合在单一机制框架内,以及按临床准备情况对生物标志物进行分层分类。
科研与实践启示
缺乏经过验证的、临床上可行的生物标志物仍然是 诊断、风险分层和有针对性的干预的主要障碍。

Emerging Biomarkers Across the Clinical Spectrum of SARS-CoV-2 Infection and Long COVID

Authors: Akhilesh Koli, Ayush Khangar, Sharmilla Khatri, Poonam V. Suryawanshi, Srikanth Tripathy
Original abstract (English)
Long COVID, also referred to in the literature as postacute sequelae of SARS-CoV-2 infection (PASC) or postacute COVID-19 syndrome (PACS), is a chronic and heterogeneous multisystem condition persisting beyond acute COVID-19. For consistency, this review uses the term “Long COVID” throughout; PASC and PACS are noted only where they reflect the terminology used by a specific cited study. The absence of validated, clinically actionable biomarkers remains a major barrier to diagnosis, risk stratification, and targeted intervention. This narrative review synthesizes evidence on candidate biomarkers relevant to SARS-CoV-2 infection, with particular attention to their mechanistic, clinical and translational relevance to long COVID specifically. Reflecting the fact that many candidate markers have been characterised primarily in acute or severe COVID-19, we explicitly indicate, for each biomarker domain, the strength and directness of the evidence linking it to long COVID as opposed to acute disease severity alone, and we distinguish exploratory candidates from those with more established clinical utility. Biomarkers are organised by domain virological, immunological/inflammatory, hematological/vascular, cardiovascular, metabolic/neuroendocrine, neurocognitive, and genetic/epigenetic beginning with virological and host-response markers, which we consider the most biologically direct evidence of ongoing SARS-CoV-2-related pathology, and closing with an integrative discussion of multiomics approaches. We also address biomarker overlap with other postinfectious and chronic inflammatory conditions, notably myalgic encephalomyelitis/chronic fatigue syndrome (ME/CFS), and the specific contribution of host genetic variation, including HLA haplotypes, to long COVID susceptibility. Relative to prior systematic reviews of long COVID biomarkers, this review’s distinguishing contribution is its explicit separation of acute-phase from chronic-phase evidence, its integration of genetic, epigenetic, and multiomics data within a single mechanistic framework, and its tiered classification of biomarkers by clinical readiness. Clinically, biomarker signatures are linked to core symptom clusters spanning respiratory, cardiovascular, neurological, metabolic and neuropsychiatric domains, underlining the potential for biomarker-informed patient classification, monitoring and targeted therapy, while emphasizing that most candidates currently remain exploratory and require validation in large, prospective, multicenter cohorts before clinical adoption.

Controversies and gaps
Relative to prior systematic reviews of long COVID biomarkers, this review’s distinguishing contribution is its explicit separation of acute-phase from chronic-phase evidence, its integration of genetic, epigenetic, and multiomics data within a single mechanistic framework, and its tiered classification of biomarkers by clinical readiness.
Research and practice implications
The absence of validated, clinically actionable biomarkers remains a major barrier to diagnosis, risk stratification, and targeted intervention.

报告既往 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染的成年人的自我评价健康状况和持续症状:一项横断面研究

Self-Rated Health and Persistent Symptoms Among Adults Reporting Previous SARS-CoV-2 Infection: A Cross-Sectional Study
作者： Dorota Weber, Robert Jan Łuczyk, Anna Beata Pacian, Patrycja Brus, Kamil Sikora, Marta Łuczyk, Anna Charuta
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本研究调查了曾感染过 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的成年人的自评健康状况与持续症状之间的关系。核心问题是当前的健康评级是否与感染前的健康评级不同,以及哪些社会人口和临床因素与这些评级相关。
证据基础与方法
A cross-sectional survey of 120 adults was conducted at University Clinical Hospital No. 4 in Lublin, Poland, and online between January and April 2026. The study used an author-designed questionnaire to assess sociodemographic and clinical characteristics, current and recalled pre-infection general health, and symptoms following coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19). Bivariate analyses were performed using non-parametric tests and chi-square tests, with p < 0.05 as the nominal significance threshold.
共识与结论
该样本中当前的健康评级不如回忆的感染前健康评级。目前最常报告的主诉是疲劳/虚弱、认知障碍(“脑雾”)和呼吸困难。当前的健康评级与年龄、教育、就业、财务状况和报告的疾病严重程度相关。平均归一化 GHQ-30 领域得分显示出中等的心理症状,财务状况较差和住院次数较多,与较差的 GHQ-30 得分呈较小的正相关。疫苗 剂量计数与一般功能有很小的相关性。
争议与缺口
该研究承认回忆偏差、选择效应以及未测量或不受控制的混杂等局限性,这些因素阻碍了将观察到的健康差异明确归因于 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染。探索性关联并未建立独立的危险因素、临床诊断或人群 患病率。该研究没有解决 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染的长期影响,也没有提供特定变体的数据。
科研与实践启示
需要进一步研究来探索 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染的长期健康影响,包括特定变异的影响。监测 和诊断工作应侧重于识别持续症状及其相关因素。公共卫生政策应考虑影响感染后人群健康结果的社会经济因素。

Self-Rated Health and Persistent Symptoms Among Adults Reporting Previous SARS-CoV-2 Infection: A Cross-Sectional Study

Authors: Dorota Weber, Robert Jan Łuczyk, Anna Beata Pacian, Patrycja Brus, Kamil Sikora, Marta Łuczyk, Anna Charuta
Original abstract (English)
Background/Objectives: Health self-assessment captures perceived physical and psychological well-being. This study described current and recalled pre-infection health ratings, persistent symptoms, and sociodemographic and clinical correlates among adults reporting previous severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 (SARS-CoV-2) infection. Methods: A cross-sectional survey of 120 adults was conducted at University Clinical Hospital No. 4 in Lublin, Poland, and online between January and April 2026. An author-designed questionnaire assessed sociodemographic and clinical characteristics, current and recalled pre-infection general health, and symptoms following coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19). The 30-item General Health Questionnaire (GHQ-30) assessed current psychological symptoms. Both general health ratings were obtained in the same survey. Bivariate analyses used non-parametric tests and chi-square tests, with p < 0.05 as the nominal significance threshold. Results: Very good health was reported by 44.2% when recalling pre-infection health and by 28.3% for current health. Current health ratings were associated with age, education, employment, financial situation, and reported disease severity (all p < 0.001). The most commonly reported current complaints were fatigue/weakness (30.8%), cognitive disturbances (‘brain fog’; 17.5%), and dyspnoea (16.7%). Mean normalised GHQ-30 domain scores were 37.1/100 for anxiety and depression, 39.0/100 for general functioning, and 30.5/100 for interpersonal relationships. Poorer financial situation and more COVID-19 hospitalisations showed small positive correlations with poorer GHQ-30 scores (R = 0.19–0.27). Vaccine dose count showed a small correlation with general functioning (R = 0.18; p = 0.045). Conclusions: Current health was rated less favourably than recalled pre-infection health in this sample. Recall bias, selection effects, and unmeasured or uncontrolled confounding prevent attribution of this difference to infection. The exploratory associations do not establish independent risk factors, clinical diagnoses, or population prevalence.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This study investigated the relationship between self-rated health and persistent symptoms among adults who had previously experienced SARS-CoV-2 infection. The central question was whether current health ratings differ from those recalled before infection and what sociodemographic and clinical factors are associated with these ratings.
Evidence base and method
A cross-sectional survey of 120 adults was conducted at University Clinical Hospital No. 4 in Lublin, Poland, and online between January and April 2026. The study used an author-designed questionnaire to assess sociodemographic and clinical characteristics, current and recalled pre-infection general health, and symptoms following coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19). Bivariate analyses were performed using non-parametric tests and chi-square tests, with p < 0.05 as the nominal significance threshold.
Consensus and conclusions
Current health ratings were less favorable than recalled pre-infection health in this sample. The most commonly reported current complaints were fatigue/weakness, cognitive disturbances (‘brain fog’), and dyspnoea. Current health ratings were associated with age, education, employment, financial situation, and reported disease severity. Mean normalised GHQ-30 domain scores indicated moderate psychological symptoms, with poorer financial situation and more hospitalisations showing small positive correlations with poorer GHQ-30 scores. Vaccine dose count showed a small correlation with general functioning.
Controversies and gaps
The study acknowledges limitations such as recall bias, selection effects, and unmeasured or uncontrolled confounding, which prevent definitive attribution of the observed health differences to SARS-CoV-2 infection. The exploratory associations do not establish independent risk factors, clinical diagnoses, or population prevalence. The study does not address the long-term effects of SARS-CoV-2 infection or provide data on specific variants.
Research and practice implications
Further research is needed to explore the long-term health impacts of SARS-CoV-2 infection, including the effects of specific variants. Surveillance and diagnostic efforts should focus on identifying persistent symptoms and their correlates. Public health policies should consider the socioeconomic factors influencing health outcomes in post-infection populations.

The Multidimensional Impact of COVID-19 and Post-COVID-19 Conditions on Sexual and Reproductive Health: A Systematic Review

The Multidimensional Impact of COVID-19 and Post-COVID-19 Conditions on Sexual and Reproductive Health: A Systematic Review
作者： Mércia Thaisa Araújo Costa Homero, Elaine dos Santos Farias, Fernanda de Souza Silva, Domenico Lopes, Sarah Alves de Assis, Iasmim Isaires Neri dos Santos, Igor Santana de Melo, Fernanda Maria Araujo de Souza, Olagide Wagner de Castro

范围与问题
由于大量的临床和方法学异质性,结果以叙述方式综合。
证据基础与方法
最终综合包括 218 份合格报告,对应于经确认的同伴/派生出版物链接后的 214 份保守研究/队列研究 单位。
共识与结论
勃起功能障碍是最一致的性结果,而生殖研究主要报告精液质量下降以及睾酮和性腺轴标记物的改变,这些改变通常是短期出现的。

然而,证据主要以男性为中心、观察性的和异质性的,限制了因果和长期的推断。

The Multidimensional Impact of COVID-19 and Post-COVID-19 Conditions on Sexual and Reproductive Health: A Systematic Review

Authors: Mércia Thaisa Araújo Costa Homero, Elaine dos Santos Farias, Fernanda de Souza Silva, Domenico Lopes, Sarah Alves de Assis, Iasmim Isaires Neri dos Santos, Igor Santana de Melo, Fernanda Maria Araujo de Souza, Olagide Wagner de Castro
Original abstract (English)
Coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19) has been associated with sexual and reproductive disturbances, but whether these findings reflect transient systemic illness or persistent dysfunction remains uncertain. We systematically searched PubMed, Scopus, Web of Science, Virtual Health Library, World Health Organization Global Index Medicus, and Embase for human studies published from 2020 through 2025, supplemented by ResearchRabbit citation- and similarity-based screening; the final update was performed in June 2026. Eligible studies assessed sexual, reproductive, psychological/behavioral, or biological outcomes in individuals with acute or post-COVID-19 conditions. Three reviewers independently extracted and verified data, using design-specific National Institutes of Health, Joanna Briggs Institute, and Mendelian-randomization-specific frameworks to assess risk of bias. Because of substantial clinical and methodological heterogeneity, results were synthesized narratively. The final synthesis included 218 eligible reports, corresponding to 214 conservative study/cohort units after confirmed companion/derived-publication linkage. Erectile dysfunction was the most consistent sexual outcome, whereas reproductive studies mainly reported reduced semen quality and alterations in testosterone and gonadal-axis markers, often appearing short-term. Inflammatory, oxidative, endocrine, vascular, histopathological, molecular, and psychosocial findings supported a multidimensional framework. However, the evidence was predominantly male-focused, observational, and heterogeneous, limiting causal and long-term inference. COVID-19 may affect sexual and reproductive health through interacting biological and psychosocial pathways, but persistent infertility or definitive reproductive impairment remains unconfirmed. The protocol was not registered; no specific external funding was received.

Multicomponent vaccination against seasonal influenza and COVID-19: a strategy to address the continued global burden of disease in adults.

Multicomponent vaccination against seasonal influenza and COVID-19: a strategy to address the continued global burden of disease in adults.
作者： W. Schaffner, S. Gravenstein, S. Kissler, O. Cornely, D. Mehta, M. G. Cenoz, Nevena Vicic, Deborah Rudin
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
季节性流感和严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2是呼吸道病毒,构成了全球健康问题,导致大量住院和死亡率,尤其是在高风险群体中,包括婴儿和老年人。
证据基础与方法
这些相关的呼吸系统疾病和相关并发症是发病率和 死亡率 的主要原因。本综述中的研究强调了多成分方法的各种潜在好处,包括简化剂量、更大的 疫苗接种 覆盖范围以及减少疾病负担和成本。
共识与结论
Although effective vaccines are available for protection against influenza and COVID-19, vaccine uptake remains below the levels recommended by the World Health Organization (WHO).
争议与缺口
增加 疫苗接种 的覆盖范围对于保护弱势群体和减轻医疗保健系统的压力至关重要。为了使多组分 疫苗接种 取得成功,关键考虑因素包括流感病毒和 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 病毒的最佳给药时机和适当的毒株选择。
科研与实践启示
成人的多组分 疫苗 方法可能会超越目前的联合给药方法,因为它有可能简化交付,减少错过 疫苗 机会,并提高 疫苗 对高危个体的覆盖率。

Multicomponent vaccination against seasonal influenza and COVID-19: a strategy to address the continued global burden of disease in adults.

Authors: W. Schaffner, S. Gravenstein, S. Kissler, O. Cornely, D. Mehta, M. G. Cenoz, Nevena Vicic, Deborah Rudin
Original abstract (English)
Seasonal influenza and SARS-CoV-2 are respiratory viruses that represent a global health concern, causing substantial numbers of hospitalizations and mortality, particularly among high-risk groups, including infants and older adults. Together these associated respiratory diseases and related complications are among the leading causes of morbidity and mortality. Although effective vaccines are available for protection against influenza and COVID-19, vaccine uptake remains below the levels recommended by the World Health Organization (WHO). Increasing vaccination coverage is critical for protecting vulnerable populations and reducing strain on healthcare systems. To improve vaccine uptake, the WHO recommends co-administering influenza and COVID-19 vaccines when epidemiologically justified. A multicomponent vaccine approach in adults could go beyond the current co-administration approach, as it has the potential to streamline delivery, reduce missed vaccination opportunities, and enhance vaccine coverage among individuals at risk. This manuscript reviews the current evidence on multicomponent vaccination for protection against seasonal influenza and COVID-19. We summarize published literature on the potential public health impacts of a multicomponent vaccination approach, discuss associated challenges, and review multicomponent vaccines currently in development. The studies in this review highlight various potential benefits of a multicomponent approach, including simplified dosing, greater vaccination coverage, and reduced disease burden and costs. Healthcare workers expressed a preference for a combination vaccine over standalone influenza or COVID-19 vaccines, indicating a high likelihood of recommending it to patients. Consumers also showed strong interest. For multicomponent vaccination to succeed, key considerations include the optimal timing of administration and appropriate strain selection for both the influenza and SARS-CoV-2 viruses. Promising multicomponent influenza-COVID-19 vaccine candidates currently being evaluated in clinical trials may represent an important advancement in adult immunization strategy, and a meaningful opportunity to strengthen protection against two of the most consequential respiratory pathogens worldwide.

Lifestyle factors or protective behaviors: which are more strongly associated with upper respiratory tract infections after COVID-19? A cross-sectional study of 1,535 Chinese college students

新闻简报The WHO COVID-19 dashboard provides a global summary of confirmed SARS-CoV-2 cases and deaths, with data collected from countries, territories, and areas. It includes information on case definitions, testing strategies, and reporting practices, which vary by region. The dashboard has been updated since March 2020, with weekly data from July 2023 onwards. Data is subject to continuous verification and may change due to retrospective updates or shifts in reporting standards. WHO acknowledges that case detection, testing, and reporting practices differ globally, which can lead to under or overestimation of true case and death counts. Additionally, some countries have stopped reporting or changed their reporting frequency, which may affect the completeness of the data. The dashboard serves as a tool for monitoring the pandemic's spread and impact, but it is important to note that the data reflects official reports and may not capture all cases or deaths accurately.
查看新闻五要素
时间
从2019年12月31日至2020年3月21日,以及自2020年3月22日起,从2023年7月起每周更新(N14、N15、N21)。
地点与对象
全球,数据收集自国家、地区和地区,包括高收入、低收入、中低收入和中上收入群体(N1、N16)。
事件
WHO monitors and reports on the global epidemiological situation of SARS-CoV-2 through the WHO COVID-19 dashboard, including confirmed cases and deaths (N1, N4, N10).
规模、影响与风险
该仪表板包括向世卫组织报告的确诊病例和死亡病例,数据需要经过验证,并且由于回顾性更新或报告做法的变化而可能发生变化(N1、N8、N18)。
应对、状态与不确定性
世卫组织收集和传播其会员国报告的数据,可能存在重大数据错误和更正,并承认各地区在病例发现、检测策略和报告做法方面存在差异(N13、N9、N18)。
A high priorityPublic-health news

COVID-19 dashboard

News Brief
The WHO COVID-19 dashboard provides a global summary of confirmed SARS-CoV-2 cases and deaths, with data collected from countries, territories, and areas. It includes information on case definitions, testing strategies, and reporting practices, which vary by region. The dashboard has been updated since March 2020, with weekly data from July 2023 onwards. Data is subject to continuous verification and may change due to retrospective updates or shifts in reporting standards. WHO acknowledges that case detection, testing, and reporting practices differ globally, which can lead to under or overestimation of true case and death counts. Additionally, some countries have stopped reporting or changed their reporting frequency, which may affect the completeness of the data. The dashboard serves as a tool for monitoring the pandemic's spread and impact, but it is important to note that the data reflects official reports and may not capture all cases or deaths accurately.

If data for certain countries is unavailable in this section, it may indicate that they have either ceased reporting COVID-19 surveillance data to WHO or have integrated the COVID-19 surveillance into existing respiratory disease surveillance. In the latter case, SARS-CoV-2 detections from sentinel and systematic virological surveillance sites in those countries may be found in the Circulation section which also includes information on SARS-CoV-2 variant circulation.
